Dispatch code-reviewer subagent:**\n\nUse Task tool with ultrapowers:code-reviewer type, fill template at `code-reviewer.md`\n\n**Placeholders:**\n- `{WHAT_WAS_IMPLEMENTED}` - What you just built\n- `{PLAN_OR_REQUIREMENTS}` - What it should do\n- `{BASE_SHA}` - Starting commit\n- `{HEAD_SHA}` - Ending commit\n- `{DESCRIPTION}` - Brief summary\n\n**3. Act on feedback:**\n- Fix Critical issues immediately\n- Fix Important issues before proceeding\n- Note Minor issues for later\n- Push back if reviewer is wrong (with reasoning)\n\n## Example\n\n```\n[Just completed Task 2: Add verification function]\n\nYou: Let me request code review before proceeding.\n\nBASE_SHA=$(git log --oneline | grep \"Task 1\" | head -1 | awk '{print $1}')\nHEAD_SHA=$(git rev-parse HEAD)\n\n[Dispatch ultrapowers:code-reviewer subagent]\n  WHAT_WAS_IMPLEMENTED: Verification and repair functions for conversation index\n  PLAN_OR_REQUIREMENTS: Task 2 from docs/superpowers/plans/deployment-plan.md\n  BASE_SHA: a7981ec\n  HEAD_SHA: 3df7661\n  DESCRIPTION: Added verifyIndex() and repairIndex() with 4 issue types\n\n[Subagent returns]:\n  Strengths: Clean architecture, real tests\n  Issues:\n    Important: Missing progress indicators\n    Minor: Magic number (100) for reporting interval\n  Assessment: Ready to proceed\n\nYou: [Fix progress indicators]\n[Continue to Task 3]\n```\n\n## Integration with Workflows\n\n**Subagent-Driven Development:**\n- Review after EACH task\n- Catch issues before they compound\n- Fix before moving to next task\n\n**Executing Plans:**\n- Review after each batch (3 tasks)\n- Get feedback, apply, continue\n\n**Ad-Hoc Development:**\n- Review before merge\n- Review when stuck\n\n## Red Flags\n\n**Never:**\n- Skip review because \"it's simple\"\n- Ignore Critical issues\n- Proceed with unfixed Important issues\n- Argue with valid technical feedback\n\n**If reviewer wrong:**\n- Push back with technical reasoning\n- Show code/tests that prove it works\n- Request clarification\n\nSee template at: requesting-code-review/code-reviewer.md\n"

\"image\" : \"file\";\n}\n\nexport function normalizeChatAttachment(value: unknown): ChatAttachment | null {\n  if (!isRecord(value)) {\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  const rawStoragePath = typeof value.storagePath === \"string\" && value.storagePath.trim()\n    ? value.storagePath.trim()\n    : typeof value.path === \"string\" && value.path.trim()\n      ? value.path.trim()\n      : undefined;\n  const fallbackName = rawStoragePath?.split(/[/\\\\]/).filter(Boolean).pop();\n  const name = typeof value.name === \"string\" && value.name.trim()\n    ? value.name.trim()\n    : fallbackName || null;\n  const id = typeof value.id === \"string\" && value.id.trim()\n    ? value.id.trim()\n    : name && rawStoragePath\n      ? `legacy-${name}-${rawStoragePath}`\n      : null;\n  const mimeType = typeof value.mimeType === \"string\" ? value.size\n    : 0;\n  const storagePath = rawStoragePath;\n\n  if (!id || !name) {\n    return null;\n  }\n\n  return {\n    id,\n    kind,\n    name,\n    mimeType,\n    size,\n    ...(storagePath ? { storagePath } : {}),\n  };\n}\n\nexport function normalizeChatAttachments(value: unknown): ChatAttachment[] {\n  if (!Array.isArray(value)) {\n    return [];\n  }\n\n  return value.map(normalizeChatAttachment).filter((attachment): attachment is ChatAttachment => Boolean(attachment));\n}\n\nexport function parseChatAttachmentsJson(value: string | null | undefined): ChatAttachment[] {\n  if (!value) {\n    return [];\n  }\n\n  try {\n    return normalizeChatAttachments(JSON.parse(value));\n  } catch {\n    return [];\n  }\n}\n\nexport function serializeChatAttachments(attachments: ChatAttachment[]): string | null {\n  const persisted = normalizeChatAttachments(attachments).map(({ id, kind, name, mimeType, size, storagePath }) => ({\n    id,\n    kind,\n    name,\n    mimeType,\n    size,\n    ...(storagePath ? { storagePath } : {}),\n  }));\n\n  return persisted.length > 0 ? JSON.stringify(persisted) : null;\n}\n\nexport function formatBytes(size: number) {\n  if (!Number.isFinite(size) || size <= 0) {\n    return \"0 B\";\n  }\n\n  const units = [\"B\", \"KB\", \"MB\", \"GB\"];\n  let value = size;\n  let unitIndex = 0;\n\n  while (value >= 1024 && unitIndex < units.length - 1) {\n    value /= 1024;\n    unitIndex += 1;\n  }\n\n  return `${value >= 10 || unitIndex === 0 ? Math.round(value) : value.toFixed(1)} ${units[unitIndex]}`;\n}\n\nexport interface AttachmentContextOptions {\n  // Map a stored attachment path to the path that should be embedded in\n  // the prompt the worker sees. Storage paths are relative to the\n  // OmniHarness data root, but the worker's cwd is the user's project,\n  // so callers on the server must pass an absolute-path resolver\n  // (typically `getAppDataPath`). Without it the worker tries to read a\n  // file that doesn't exist under its cwd.\n  resolvePath?: (storagePath: string) => string;\n  // True when the caller also delivers image attachments as real image\n  // content blocks on the same prompt (see `resolveImageAttachments`).\n  // The worker receives both the pixels for visual understanding and the\n  // saved path for work that needs the original file (for example, turning\n  // an uploaded logo into project assets).\n  imagesInlined?: boolean;\n}\n\nexport function formatAttachmentContext(\n  attachments: ChatAttachment[],\n  options: AttachmentContextOptions = {},\n) {\n  const normalized = normalizeChatAttachments(attachments).filter((attachment) => attachment.storagePath);\n  if (normalized.length === 0) {\n    return \"\";\n  }\n\n  const resolvePath = options.resolvePath ?? ((p: string) => p);\n  const inlineImages = options.imagesInlined === true;\n  const sections: string[] = [];\n\n  const images = normalized.filter((attachment) => attachment.kind === \"image\");\n  const files = normalized.filter((attachment) => attachment.kind !== \"image\");\n\n  if (images.length > 0) {\n    const lines = images.map((attachment) => [\n      `- ${attachment.name}`,\n      `mime: ${attachment.mimeType || \"unknown\"}`,\n      `size: ${formatBytes(attachment.size)}`,\n      `path: ${resolvePath(attachment.storagePath!)}`,\n    ].join(\" | \"));\n    sections.push(\n      inlineImages\n        ? `Attached images (included directly in this message):\\n${lines.join(\"\\n\")}`\n        : `Attached images, readable from disk at the paths shown:\\n${lines.join(\"\\n\")}`,\n    );\n  }\n\n  if (files.length > 0) {\n    const lines = files.map((attachment) => [\n      `- ${attachment.name}`,\n      `kind: ${attachment.kind}`,\n      `mime: ${attachment.mimeType || \"unknown\"}`,\n      `size: ${formatBytes(attachment.size)}`,\n      `path: ${resolvePath(attachment.storagePath!)}`,\n    ].join(\" | \"));\n    sections.push(`Attached files available to inspect:\\n${lines.join(\"\\n\")}`);\n  }\n\n  return sections.join(\"\\n\\n\");\n}\n\nexport function appendAttachmentContext(\n  content: string,\n  attachments: ChatAttachment[],\n  options: AttachmentContextOptions = {},\n) {\n  const context = formatAttachmentContext(attachments, options);\n  const trimmedContent = content.trim();\n\n  if (!context) {\n    return trimmedContent;\n  }\n\n  return trimmedContent ? `${trimmedContent}\\n\\n${context}` : context;\n}\n\nexport interface ResolvedImageAttachment {\n  path: string;\n  mimeType: string;\n}\n\n// Resolve image attachments to absolute paths so a caller can read them and\n// attach them as image content blocks. Callers on the server pass\n// `getAppDataPath` as the resolver, matching `formatAttachmentContext`.\nexport function resolveImageAttachments(\n  attachments: ChatAttachment[],\n  resolvePath: (storagePath: string) => string,\n): ResolvedImageAttachment[] {\n  return normalizeChatAttachments(attachments)\n    .filter((attachment) => attachment.kind === \"image\" && attachment.storagePath)\n    .map((attachment) => ({\n      path: resolvePath(attachment.storagePath!),\n      mimeType: attachment.mimeType || \"image/png\",\n    }));\n}\n"

## Codex progress · 2026-08-02T19:57:53.035Z

The core change is small and keeps the existing inline image argument intact. I’m checking the surrounding delivery code now because the main production risk is whether every route uses the same trusted saved path—not just whether the formatter prints one.

## Codex progress · 2026-08-02T20:03:24.530Z

The saved-session retry itself is correctly wired, including the code branch that builds a transcript-replay prompt. I found a separate retry fallback that still drops attachment data when no saved session is available, and I’m confirming the tests don’t accidentally hide that case.

## Codex final · 2026-08-02T20:03:53.671Z

Updated verdict: **Not ready; one blocking recovery gap remains.**

Important/blocking:

- [src/server/runs/recovery.ts:345](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/runs/recovery.ts:345) drops all attachment data in the fresh-worker retry fallback. When `resumeDirectRunFromSavedSession()` returns `null` because session metadata is missing ([recovery.ts:1035](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/runs/recovery.ts:1035)), `startDirectRerun()` receives only plain text. It neither adds the saved image path nor passes the image object to `askAgent`. A canceled image turn without usable session metadata therefore still reproduces the bug.

Test gap:

- [tests/api/run-route.test.ts:1814](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/tests/api/run-route.test.ts:1814) covers only successful saved-session resume, not transcript replay.
- Its prompt assertion at [tests/api/run-route.test.ts:1819](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/tests/api/run-route.test.ts:1819) checks only `path:` plus the relative storage path, so it would not catch an incorrect absolute root. Assert the complete `path: ${getAppDataPath(attachment.storagePath)}`.
- The existing missing-session fallback test at [tests/api/run-route.test.ts:2250](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/tests/api/run-route.test.ts:2250) should also carry an image and verify both prompt path and image argument. A true transcript-replay test should force materialized-session resume to fail, then verify the same pair.

Strength: the new saved-session and transcript-replay code at [recovery.ts:695](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/runs/recovery.ts:695) and [recovery.ts:784](/Users/masterman/NLP/omniharness/src/server/runs/recovery.ts:784) correctly keeps the path-bearing prompt and image object together.

Verification: all 111 targeted tests pass, but they do not exercise the blocking fallback above.

## Codex final · 2026-08-02T20:06:53.806Z

Final verdict: **Ready for production.**

No remaining Critical or Important issues found.

Verified:

- `startDirectRerun()` now preserves attachment metadata in the worker stream and sends both the absolute saved path and image object.
- Both fresh-worker callers pass `targetAttachments`.
- Forked messages retain `attachmentsJson`, so copied history and the rerun target remain consistent.
- Saved-session and transcript-replay paths converge on the same `askAgent` call with resolved images; replay prompts are built from the path-bearing message prompt.
- The missing-session regression asserts the complete absolute path and image argument.
- All 111 targeted tests passed.
- TypeScript typecheck and `git diff --check` passed.

Non-blocking improvement: the successful saved-session test could assert the full absolute prompt path, and a dedicated forced transcript-replay image test would provide stronger branch-specific coverage. The current implementation is nevertheless correct across those paths.
